115 年 10 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
10 月出口 967.6 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 10.9%，較上
年同月增 58.9%(按新臺幣計算增 66.6%)；累 計1 至 10 月出口 7,582.9
億美元，較上年同期增 47.7%(按新臺幣計算增 51.1%)。 
（二）進口 
10 月進口 688.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 8.3%，較上
年同月增 75.1%(按新臺幣計算增 83.5%)；累 計1 至 10 月進口 5,697.6
億美元，較上年同期增 44.6%(按新臺幣計算增 47.6%)。 
（三）出入超 
10 月出超 278.8 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 63.3 億美元；
累計 1 至 10 月出超 1,885.4 億美元，較上年同期增 689.7 億美元。

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、 高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續拓展，
加上適逢傳統外銷旺季，以及價格攀升效應，10 月出口較上年同月
增 358.7 億美元(+58.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 476.0 億美元(歷年單月新高)， 較上年同月增
200.7 億美元(+72.9%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增 151.6 億美元
(+67.5%)、 電腦之零附件增18.5 億美元(+1.0 倍)、 儲存媒體增17.3
億美元(+2.1 倍)、交換器及路由器增 11.7 億美元(+1.3 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 326.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)， 較上年同月增124.3
億美元(+61.4%)，其中積體電路增 120.5 億美元(+63.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.9 億美元(+38.5%)、 電容器及電阻器增1.2 億美元(+48.6%)。 
3. 基本金屬及其製品出口 28.8 億美元，較上年同月增 7.6 億美元
(+35.7%)，其中銅及其製品增 4.7 億美元(+1.2 倍)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.9 億美元(+16.4%)。 
4. 機械出口 28.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增 7.5 億美元
(+35.5%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.9 億美元(+57.5%)、機器、
工廠或實驗室設備增 1.4 億美元(+70.0%)。 
5. 礦產品出口 15.5 億美元，較上年同月增 6.5 億美元(+71.6%)，其
中石油煉製品增 5.7 億美元(+72.2%)。

（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月出口較上年同期增 2,448.3 億美元(+47.7%)，以資通
與視聽產品增 1,423.3 億美元(+72.5%)、電子零組件增 850.5 億美元
(+47.5%)增加較多，礦產品、機械亦各增 41.9 億美元(+36.8%)、41.5
億美元(+19.8%)。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路、資通產品
及半導體設備進口持續強勁，加上國際原物料行情居高，10 月總進
口較上年同月增 295.5 億美元(+75.1%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口458.3 億美元， 較上年同月增195.0億美元(+74.0%)，
其中電子零組件增 136.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)、礦產品增 22.8 億美元
(+58.0%)、電腦之零附件增 16.2 億美元(+70.0%)、基本金屬增 6.2
億美元(+33.4%)、塑化原料增 5.0 億美元(+22.0%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 163.4 億美元， 較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通設備增 46.9 億美元(+2.0 倍)、半導體設備增 22.4 億美元
(+83.4%)、交通運輸設備增 7.6 億美元(+7.0 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 61.1 億美元，較上年同月增 18.8 億美元(+44.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.0 億美元(+5.3 倍)、食品增 0.5 億
美元(+5.1%)、小客車增 0.4 億美元(+10.2%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
10 月對美國、歐洲、日本出口創新高 
1. 對美國出口 329.3 億美元，較上年同月增 118.1 億美元(+55.9%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 98.3 億美元(+56.6%)、電子零組件增 13.5
億美元(+1.7 倍)、機械增 2.4 億美元(+41.6%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 204.4 億美元，較上年同月增 61.4 億美元
(+42.9%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+48.0%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.6 億美元(+36.5%) 、基本金屬及其製品增 3.2 億美元
(+95.2%)。 
3. 對東協出口 184.6 億美元，較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+83.8%)，
其中電子零組件增 38.6 億美元(+75.5%)、資通與視聽產品增 36.5
億美元(+1.4 倍)、礦產品增 5.0 億美元(+2.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 59.5 億美元，較上年同月增 18.7 億美元(+46.0%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 10.7 億美元(+47.9%)、電子零組件增 3.3 億
美元(+81.3%)、機械增 1.2 億美元(+50.8%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.7 億美元，較上年同月增 12.2 億美元(+49.6%)，其
中電子零組件增 7.5 億美元(+63.2%)、資通與視聽產品增 3.2 億美
元(+1.3 倍)、化學品增 0.5 億美元(+47.0%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月對美國出口 2,407.1 億美元 ， 較上年同期增891.1 億美
元(+58.8%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,805.3 億美元，增 416.6 億美
元(+30.0%)， 對東協出口1,524.1 億美元 ， 增547.6 億美元(+56.1%)，
對歐洲出口 481.3 億美元 ， 增157.1 億美元(+48.5%)， 對日本出口314.4
億美元，增 70.5 億美元(+28.9%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
10 月自東協進口 134.1 億美元 ，較上年同月增 82.3 億美元(+1.6 倍)，
自南韓進口 130.0 億 美 元，增73.1 億美元(+1.3 倍)， 自中國大陸與香
港進口 128.3 億美元，增 51.9 億美元(+67.9%)，自日本進口 62.0 億
美元，增 18.8 億美元(+43.4%)，自美國進口 53.6 億美元，增 14.7 億
美元(+37.9%)，自歐洲進口 48.2 億美元，增 9.5 億美元(+24.6%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計1 至10 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增85.6%、
自南韓增 72.7%、自中國大陸與香港增 45.3%、自日本增 27.4%、自
美國增 23.0%、自歐洲增 12.1%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-9 月主要國家/地區出口均呈上升，其中南韓(1-10 月)、香港、
新加坡受 AI 相關需求帶動科技供應鏈及區域貿易活動熱絡，累計出
口分別年增 60.6%、52.3%、41.3%；中國大陸、美國各年增 21.3%
及 14.3%，德國(1-8 月)、日本各增 8.8%、8.3%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步成長，累計 1-9 月以香港進口年增 51.8%較
高，新加坡、 中國大陸、南 韓(1-10 月)分別年增 39.0%、27.5%及 21.4%，
德國(1-8 月)、日本、美國各增 8.4%、8.2%、6.7%。

### Page 7

7
圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）隨全球人工智慧(AI)基礎建設投資強勁成長，帶動相關供應鏈需求擴
增，加以外銷產品價格攀升，10 月出口達 967.6 億美元，首度突破
900 億美元，年增 58.9%，連續 36 個月正成長；進口688.9 億美元，
同為歷年單月最高，年增 75.1%，主要受惠於 AI 產業之國際分工與
出口引申需求增加，以及國際原物料行情高漲；出超 278.8 億美元亦
創新高。累計 1 至 10 月出口年增 47.7%、進口增 44.6%。
（二）10 月出口主要貨品中，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件出口值雙創單月
新高，各年增 72.9%及 61.4%，主因 AI、高效能運算及雲端服務等應
用商機熱絡；其餘貨類出口平均增 25.7%，其中礦產品因油價支撐增
71.6%，機械、電機產品受惠於半導體業者擴充產能及AI 需求外溢，
增幅皆達 3 成， 基本金屬及其製品亦增35.7%。累 計1 至 10 月資通與
視聽產品、電子零組件合占總出口比重約 8 成，年增 60.6%。

（三）10 月對主要市場出口年增率均逾 4 成，以對東協增 83.8%較突出，
對美國、日本、歐洲出口規模皆為單月新高，各增 55.9%、49.6%及
46.0%， 對中國大陸與香港增42.9%。累 計1 至 10 月對五大市場出口
增幅介於 28%~59%， 其中對美國出口年增 58.8%、對東協增 56.1%，
占總出口比重各為 31.7%及 20.1%，分別寫下近 36 年同期及歷年同
期新高紀錄。
（四）展望未來，全球經濟仍面臨地緣政治 衝突、美國貿易政策走向等不
確定因素，惟隨人工智慧應用逐漸擴散至推論、代理式 AI，且國際
雲端服務業者持續布建 AI 基礎設施，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供
應鏈需求延續，加以科技新品相繼推 出、年底消費旺季來臨之挹注，
我國第 4 季出口可望維持強勁成長態勢。

營收走勢圖

研究員/吳大任 TERRY.WU@uni-psg.com

研究部對義隆電的投資建議維持中立 ， 係因(1)受到產業影響 ，3Q26
財報大幅不如預期 ，(2)4Q26 淡季更淡 ， 預估下修幅度不小 ，(3)27
年產業狀況仍受到 CPU、記憶體等元件影響，目前較難樂觀看待，
(4)下修 26、27 年獲利預估。綜合以上，下修義隆電 26 年 EPS 預
估為 6.26 元(原估 9.37 元，-22%YOY)，及預估 27 年 EPS 為 6.58
元(原估 10.14 元，+5.1%YOY)，因為 NB 市場除了先前的記憶體影
響外 ， 目前更受到CPU 缺貨的影響 ， 短線上義隆電的營運動能受到
連帶影響大 ， 靜待無人機相關IC 營收在 2H27 放大 ， 故投資建議維
持中立，目標價 132 元。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營收大幅滑落，獲利不如預期： 
義隆電 3Q26 營收僅為 22.2 億元(原估 30.85 億元)，衰退
30.7%QOQ，衰退33%YOY，營收大規模衰退，除了記憶體漲
價外，因為Agent AI 興起，使得CPU 缺貨嚴重，另外因為
成本反映至終端產品，故使得需求更是急凍。毛利率僅
44.7%，相對於2Q26 下滑 2.7 個百分點，主要就是各類成
本漲價，包含晶圓代工、封裝及 TOUCH PAD 內的 PCB 及被
動元件等，另外產品組合也轉差所致。另外業外又有高達
1 億元的金融評價損失，故使得稅後淨利僅為 1.68 億元
(原估 6.23 億元)，衰退 80.9%QOQ，衰退 79.1%YOY，EPS 
0.55 元(原估 EPS 2.05 元)。3Q26 產品組合為：AI 14%，
觸控 73%， 非觸控13%， 其中TOUCH PAD 43%，TOUCH SCREEN 
14%，指紋 16%，POINT STICK 19%，MCU+SENSOR 4%。

2. 3Q26 未給展望，但受到 CPU 缺貨影響嚴重： 
義隆電因為最近四季合併（個別）財務報表營業外收支淨
額合計數占稅前損益合計數之絕對值若大於 10%，在法說
會中不能提及預測性資訊，但在法說會有提到 4Q26 本來
就是季節性淡季，另外客戶在上半年非常積極拉貨，下半
年開始調整庫存，故 4Q26 推估各產線仍有下滑，僅有 AI
相關產品會成長。故研究部下修 4Q26 營收預估為 16.5 億
元(原估 31.7 億元)，衰退25.7%QOQ，衰退42.3%YOY，稅
後淨利同步下修預估為 1.51 億元(原估 6.38 億元)，衰退
10.1%QOQ， 衰退79.5%YOY，EPS 0.5 元(原估 EPS 2.1 元)。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

3. 對 27 年 NB 產業看法仍未定，但義隆仍有新商機： 
義隆電在法說會表示，NB 產業有各研調機構都有看法，目
前普遍較為保守，但未來仍端視 27 年記憶體、各元件及
CPU 的狀況，非單一供應商決定市況，故認為在此階段仍
不易下結論。但義隆電在 NB 觸控相關 IC 方面，因為美系
競爭對手被併購後 ， 義隆電有SHARE GAIN 部分市場 ， 另外
也提供更廣泛的產品選項供客戶選擇，故公司認為本身的
NB 相關 IC 出貨量 27 年將優於市場表現。

4. 無人機貢獻性確實提升，大貢獻性將落在 2H27： 
1H26 無人機營收占比約低個位數，3Q26 已經達到 14%，
4Q26 有望在持續攀升。目前主要配合的客戶為國內廠商，
該國內廠商前天六項標案全數得標，而義隆電現在主要出
貨的產品為 CAMERA 模組，目前為兩個鏡頭一個模組 +AI 
BOX，單價為數十美元，目前送樣 4 鏡頭+避障+AI BOX 的
解決方案，單價高達 800 美元，有望在 1H27 逐步開始量
產，下半年完整貢獻。推估 AI 產品營收在 27 年成長約
30%YOY 以上。

5. 大幅下修26年預估，27年預估也較為保守：  
3Q26受到客戶已經提前拉貨影響，加上相關零組件及封裝
成本提升，使得3Q26營收及獲利大幅不如預期，加上4Q26
又有下修，故研究部下修26年義隆電營收預估為103.02億
元(原估126.8億元)，EPS下修預估至6.26元(原估EPS 9.37
元)。 展望27年 ， 目前研究部以27年NB整體出貨量高雙位數
衰退，但義隆電整體為低雙位數衰退，雖然義隆電無人機
相關產品推進至整合型產品，單價大幅提升下，仍下修27
年營收預估為112.3億元(原估140億元)， 成長9%YOY， 稅後
淨利下修預估為20億元(原估30.8億元)，成長5.1%YOY，
EPS 6.58元(原估10.14元)。

Document 139: 261109_daiwa_ Giant Manufacturing

Source PDF: all report pdf/261109_daiwa_ Giant Manufacturing.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-09

### Page 1

Taiwan Consumer Discretionary

What’s new: Giant announced its 3Q26 results after market hours on 9 
November 2026. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating on its improving 
fundamentals and the recovery of the global bike market. We believe 
inventory destocking is coming to an end.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results: missed on operating margin. Giant’s 
3Q26 revenue of TWD16.311bn (-2.1% QoQ, +6.2% YoY) was roughly in 
line with our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus. Its gross margin of 
23.9% beat our estimate and the Bloomberg consensus by 2.9pp and 
1.1pp, respectively, driven by product mix optimisation and new product 
launches in its own brand. However, its operating margin of 3.1% missed 
our estimate and the consensus by 1.4pp and 2.1pp, respectively, due to 
marketing activities for MY27 launches and legal fees related to the WRO. 
Thus, its 3Q26 operating profit of TWD510m (-49.0% QoQ, +28.9% YoY) 
missed our estimate and the consensus by 31.3% and 42.2%, respectively. 
Its 3Q26 net profit of TWD330m (-48.0% QoQ, -5.7% YoY) also missed our 
estimate and the consensus by 39.6% and 47.8%, respectively. Its 3Q26 
EPS finished at TWD0.84.

Demand recovery in Europe and China; a bright 2027. Giant sees 
gradual market recovery in Europe on new model launches and demand 
recovery in China. In the US, Giant continues to work towards having the 
WRO lifted, but will adjust production allocation to maximise the supply to 
meet demand in the US market. Its E-bike revenue accounted for 21% of 
9M26 revenue, which we believe reflects the declining OEM segment. 
Giant aims for its e-bike shipments to decline by 1% YoY, from 516,000 
units in 2025 to 510,000 in 2026, on weaker OE customer demand. For 
2026, it guides for sales to decline by low-single-digit % YoY, with the gross 
margin improving to 21-23% (from 20-21%) and an operating margin of 1-
3%. We lower our 2026-28E EPS by 5-30% after factoring in the 3Q26 
results with more conservative operating-margin estimates on marketing 
expenses.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) call with an unchanged 
12M TP of TWD121, still based on our 1-year forward EPS forecast with an 
unchanged target PER of 22x to reflect its improved earnings trajectory, 
and we look for a better year in 2027. Key downside risks: slower-than-
expected e-bike penetration in the EU and macro/tariff uncertainties.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 8-26% below the consensus, likely 
as our revenue and operating margin forecasts are more conservative.

9 November 2026

3Q26 results review: missed on operating margin

➢ 3Q26 net profit missed our estimate and the consensus by 39-48% 
➢ Better-than-expected recovery in Europe and China; a brighter 2027 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating with an unchanged 12M TP of TWD121

Company profile 
Founded in 1972 and listed in 1994 on Taiwan’s main board, Giant is the largest bicycle maker in 
Taiwan, with an annual capacity of c.5m bicycles. In 2025, Asia accounted for 57% of its revenue, 
followed by Europe (33%) and America (7%). Around 74% of its 2025 revenue came from OBM (Giant, 
Liv, momentum) brands and 26% from OEM/ODM (Trek, Scott and Colnago) brands.

### Page 5

5

Giant Manufacturing (9921 TT): 9 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 7 Aug 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and Chairperson are not the same individual. Led by the Liu family, there are 11
members on the board. The founder and former Chairman is now a director, with his niece, Mrs.
Tu, leading Giant. We view Mrs. Tu's leadership to be strong, as she is well-recognised by the
former Chairman and the board for her capabilities, and she is one of the few female
chairpersons to lead a Taiwanese company.
Capital management 2 Giant has maintained its dividend payout ratio between 50% and 100% over the past decade , which we believe to be reasonable considering its global leadership status and expansion needs.
Related party &
transaction 2
Revenue from related-party transactions accounted for 58-62% of total revenue over 2019-25,
with sales mostly from its OBM (ASP varies with products/markets). We observed no suspicious
activity.
S Product quality & safety 1
According to Giant's 2023 ESG report, Giant as the global OEM/OBM leader in the bicycle
industry, product quality and safety are the top priority. We see ongoing R&D expenses and
relevant investment in new models and note that Giant has introduced its "Right Ride" system to
assist customers seeking suitable bike options.
S Supply chain management 1
Giant has developed its Giant production management system that diversifies its capacity
globally and utilises production resources fully to lower political or regional risk. As Giant has
control over its material sourcing, design process and manufacturing/OEM, we believe it has
solid supply chain management capabilities that allow for better cost control than peers.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

ZDT 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 10%/11% higher than GSe and 
BBG consensus, mainly thanks to the better than expected product 
mix (high margin & high growth sectors accounted for 27% of total 
revenue in 3Q26 vs. our estimate only at 20-25%). In 3Q26, ZDT GM 
was 1.8/2.6ppt higher than GSe/BBG consensus, and was 27.7% up 
4.6ppt QoQ and 5.7ppt YoY, while the annual growth was only 
driven by the better product mix/ new business contribution 
(ABF/optical module/AI PCB), even considering these new 
businesses only accounted for 27% of total revenue.  
OPEX in 3Q26 increased 24% QoQ and 56% YoY due mainly to the 
higher investment in the R&D projects for future new high-end AI 
PCB/ optical transceiver / substrates business, which was the main 
reason for the company to see a 0.7/1.4ppt higher than GSe/BBG 
consensus OPEX to sales ratio in 3Q26.  
In 4Q26, we expect ZDT revenue to go up by ~29% QoQ, mainly due 
to the solid growth from the optical transceiver, substrates, and AI 
PCB businesses, with more new AI projects to ramp in 4Q26. 
Moreover, we expect the company’s GM to further expand to ~30% 
(2.2ppt QoQ or 7.3ppt YoY) thanks to a better product mix (we 
expect high growth/high margin new business will account for 
30-35% of the company’s total revenue in 4Q26, whose average GM 
should be at least 40-45% in 4Q26).  
Investment view 
We remain positive on ZDT’s AI business outlook, and expect the 
acceleration on ZDT’s AI PCB/Substrates business revenue to further 
drive the company’s overall GM level in coming years (we expect the 
two businesses’ average GM could surpass 50% in 2027, thanks to 
the favorable supply demand condition) while we also expect the 
high GM optical transceiver (50%+ GM now) will also see at least 
120% YoY growth in 2027, suggesting a significant GM expansion 
trend for the overall company in coming quarters/years.

BUY
Zhen Ding Technology Holding (4958.TW)For the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM
e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 3

We believe ZDT will continue to be the key share gainer in the AI PCB market. For AI GPU 
projects, we are seeing ZDT share increase from <5% in 2025 to ~10-15% in 2026, 
which, we believe, will further go up to 30%+ in long term (2028 and beyond), thanks to 
the company’s proactive investment in the R&D on the latest PCB technology, as well as 
the company’s solid investment in the AI grade PCB capacity. For AI ASIC projects, the 
company’s market share is well below 10% for now, and only focus on some minor 
projects, but we do expect the company to become one of the major suppliers for AI 
ASIC projects in coming years, considering the company’s more diversified production 
sites (mainland China + Taiwan + Thailand), while most of the AI ASIC customers are 
asking for non-Greater China capacity now.  
For the optical transceiver business, we expect ZDT to see strong growth (50%+ 
2025-29E CAGR), driven mainly by the ~50% 2026-28E volume demand CAGR and the 
company’s proactive expansion in capacity in coming years (5x more capacity over the 
next two years). Moreover, ZDT currently holds an industry-leading yield of 90%+ for its 
optical module mSAP products (vs. peers only at 60-80% per our industry checks).  
For its ABF substrate business, we believe the company will continue to have the ability 
to increase ABF price, supported by full UTR and strong demand, while the industry in 
our view remains conservative on its capacity expansion plans over the next 1-2 years 
relative to our forecasts. We expect ABF substrate LTA/Spot prices to increase by 
10-15%/20%+ QoQ per quarter from 3Q26 through at least the end of 2027, with 
shortages likely extending into 2028 (see here). Overall, the business condition for ABF 
substrate is improving with price increase and we expect the business, which was loss 
making in 2025, to expand its GM to 40%+/50%+ by end of 2026/27. 
Overall, we reiterate our Buy rating on ZDT, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,070 (from 
NT$1,025).

Earnings revisions 
We revise up our 2026/27/28/29E net income estimate by 6/21/42/49% after seeing 
the solid GM expansion from these high-growth sectors, which we believe will continue 
to be the net income driver in the coming years. We believe the high growth sectors (AI 
PCB, substrates, optical module mSAP) will account for 50%+ of total revenue in 2028 
and further expand to 60%+ in 2029, with a much higher than company average GM 
(50%+ in average in 2027-28), thus we revise up our 2027/28/29E GM by 
5.4/10.7/12.4ppt vs. previous estimate.

### Page 4

Valuation 
We revise up ZDT 12m TP from NT$1,025 to NT$1,070, still based on our SOTP based 
valuation methodology of (1) 3.5x blended P/B for ABF & BT substrate (in-line with 
upcycle industry average), (2) 15x P/E for high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in 
line with industry average), and (3) 8x P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB 
(in-line with industry downcycle average valuation), and maintain our valuation period at 
4Q26-3Q27, factoring in our earnings revision.

Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in 
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in 
Shenzhen, China, and has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market 
compared to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% 
ABF substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the 
most from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025E-27E CAGR) 
due to the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. 
We also expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to 
drive ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 50% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We 
see further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due 
to our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which 
should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long 
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth 
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+ 
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China 
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

More details on SBC — SBC's 9M26 sales declined by a single digit y/y with stable 
GPM and improving opex. Pre-tax profits rose 40% y/y in 9M26. Yet the higher 
effective tax rate dragged profitability, leading to a  net loss of US$25mn in 9M26. 
Mgmt expects SBC to launch more new products in 2027E which should further 
drive the top-line and earnings recovery.  
Implications — We expect a negative share price reaction to the results print given 
the weaker-than-expected core biz and the SBC loss. Mgmt expects FY26 sales to 
fall 14-15% y/y with 4Q OEM sales seasonally softer than 3Q. New models are due 
from Jan-27 and mgmt expects low-teens growth y/y for FY27E. We will look for 
more detail on the drivers of the expected sales recovery at its 3Q26 results briefing 
next Tuesday, Nov 17.

Merida
Valuation
Our target price for Merida shares of NT$93 is based on a 2H26-1H27E P/E of 15.5x, a 30% discount to its historical average of
22x during pre-Covid years (2014-2019), in view of soft order momentum as a result of slow recovery in bike demand, a volatile
margin trend, and low visibility of SBC.
Risks
Downside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade below our target price include: 1) e-bike adoption slower than

expected; 2) weaker restocking demand; 3) lukewarm sell-through at SBC; and 4) a weaker macro.
Upside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade above our target price include: 1) e-bike adoption faster than expected;
2) stronger restocking demand; 3) stronger sell-through at SBC; and 4) a strong macro.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned

### Page 6

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY) 으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY) 으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY) 으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY) 을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000 원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-30

Status: ok

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대 
예정보다 빠른 SK 하이닉스 자사주 매입 
SK스퀘어의 자회사인 SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 20일부터 11월 19일까지 자사주 40
조원(2,407만주, 3.3%)을 매입 예정이다. 9월 29일까지 1,715만주, 예정 매입수량 
대비 71.3%를 매입 완료하였다. 매입 초기 매일 65만주 매입하다가 9월 10일부터는 
60만주를 매입하고 있는데, 이러한 추세가 유지된다면 10월 중순경 자사주 매입이 마
무리될 예정이다. 자사주 매입 후 소각 예정으로 소각이 완료되면 SK스퀘어의 SK하아
닉스에 대한 지분율은 20.0%에서 20.7%로 상승하게 된다.

자사주 매입 이후 추가적인 주주환원에 대한 기대 
SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 19일 정책기간(‘25년~’27년) 동안 발생하는 누적 FCF의 
50% 이상을 주주환원 하는 주주환원 정책을 발표한 바 있다. 26년 SK하이닉스의 예
상 FCF는 164조원(컨센서스 기준)으로 50%를 주주환원 할 경우 자사주 매입 금액
(40 조원)을 제외한 주주환원 재원은 42 조원이다. 잔여 주주환원 재원이 모두 배당으
로 지급된다면 SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 20.7%이므로 
SK스퀘어의 배당수입도 8.7조원에 달한다. 지난 3월 SK스퀘어는 ‘26년~’28년 경
상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 정책을 발표했다. 보수적으로 30%만 
가정하더라도 SK스퀘어의 배당재원은 2.6조원(현 시가총액 대비 1.8%)이다. SK스퀘
어는 연초 주주총회에서 배당가능이익 확보 차원에서 자본준비금 5 조 8,900억원을 
이익잉여금으로 전입한 바 있다. 이는 26년 재무제표가 확정되는 27년 3월 이후 배당
가능이익으로 편입되므로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 확대가 기대된다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 185 만원(유지) 
SK스퀘어의 순자산 가치 중 98% 이상이 SK하이닉스 지분가치이다. SK하이닉스는 
자사주 매입 이후 17.7% 주가가 상승했으나, SK 스퀘어 주가는 11.4% 상승했다. SK
하이닉스 자사주 매입·소각은 SK 스퀘어의 보유 지분가치 상승뿐 아니라 지분율도 상
승하기 때문에 자사주가 소각될 경우 SK스퀘어의 주가가 탄력적으로 움직일 가능성이 
높다. SK스퀘어 현재 NAV 대비 할인율은 43.7%, 12MF PBR 1.4배로 7월 이후 주
가 조정으로 밸류에이션 매력도 높아졌다. 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 185만원을 유지
한다.

2026-09-30

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

SK하이닉스 주주환원 재원 82,206  FCF의 50% 가정 
자사주 매입금액 40,004  (8월 20일~11월 19일)공시 예정금액

SK스퀘어 지분율 20.7% SK하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후

Document 281: An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-13).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
An RBA peak of greater conviction
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
08:56 November 13 2026 Share
We already believed the RBA cash rate was likely at a peak, even if RBA officials were less
convinced. A deeper housing downturn and early signs of cracks in the consumer sector
point to an even softer 2027. Fading inflation risks from oil also boost our conviction on
rates being at a peak.

### Page 2

It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking the cash rate after the November
increase. Since then, our conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we will publish
the November Market Outlook with revised forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing
market have all been revised down in 2027. Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for calendar 2027 more decisively inside the

RBA’s 2–3% target range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment rate forecast has
been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash
rate from here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.
A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The housing market has undershot our
forecasts over the past couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been coming in
under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan inquiries have taken another leg down in October after

the September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior months, with the additional impact
of the November rate hike still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer. Consumer income and spending have
so far been quite resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest rates. But our Westpac
Card Tracker is starting to show that discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite weak. We would not over-emphasise

this point, but it does imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB) meeting, business survey data
has shown weaker conditions and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show further
evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-labour cost shock that is not being fully
passed through to consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil and fuel prices are already materially

lower than the track assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-fourth lower than
on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of fuel costs to other prices, as well as
easing consumer inflation perceptions and expectations.
In the media conference following the November meeting, Governor Bullock flagged that it was a

good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the market read this as a
signal that the MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to downside risks than the internal
members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is looking even less likely given signs
in the data and surveys. The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and senior

officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though, a rate hike in 2027 would require
either a material new inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would constitute a policy
error on the part of the RBA (something that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the
talents and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with the dissenting voters in the MPB

that the downside risks need more attention. Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to February, we think there will be more
evidence that the domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation outlook with it.

近三月(%)
► 美股表現相對強勁 ，主要受科技 與半導體上
漲帶動 。其中標普 500與那斯達克指數一度
創高，費半指數漲勢則更為顯著。反映9月非
農數據放緩推升 10月聯準會按兵不動預期所
帶來的激勵效果延續 。另一方面 ，歐股則是
不漲反跌 ，因傳出法國債務風險疑慮與政局
不穩等 消息，帶動法國股市下跌 。日股在半
導體與金融聯袂反彈下，推動指數一度收復7

萬點整數關卡 ，港股則在陸股國慶假期休市
量能縮減下，指數亦表現疲弱。
► 產業 方面 ，馬斯 克 稱正與 台 積電 討 論 參與
Terafab 德州晶片計畫 ，加上超微表示將於
2027年大幅增加晶片供應 ，以因應AI產品需
求快速成長 ，執行長蘇姿 丰更赴 台與供應鏈
夥伴討論擴充 CPU與GPU產能，顯示AI半導
體長期發展趨勢強勁 ，激勵科技相關類股表
現。此外，G7釋放緊急原油與柴油庫存後 ，
減輕市場對能源價格與通膨的部分 擔憂，亦
帶動非必需消費產業上漲。

近三月(%)
► 美國9月非農數據公布後一度令 10年期公債
殖利率出現明顯回落 。然觀察最新 ISM服務
業之價 格分 項 指 數 較上月 出現反彈 ， 並創
2022年7月以來 最高水準 ，反映運輸 及原物
料成本上升 ，顯示通膨壓力仍可能持續向其
他產業傳導 ，公債殖利率因而再度反彈並持
續於高檔徘迴 ，多數債券市場在利率高檔震
盪環境下亦呈現跌多漲少現象。
► 受法國債務問題與西班牙提前大選等歐洲 政
治與經濟消息影響 ，歐元走勢疲弱 。儘管近
期日圓於日銀9月升息後不升反貶，然市場亦
擔憂持續走貶可能再度引發干預 ，令短線日
圓呈現區間震盪走勢。
► G7宣布透過國際能源總署協調釋放 1億桶柴
油及原油等緊急 儲備，令油價短線回落 ，然
隨中東衝突前景未明 ，供應不穩之變數短期
難解，多空因素交錯恐使油價於高檔進行反
覆波動。

► 美國經濟仍具韌性 ，惟通膨壓力猶存 。觀察
美國9月ISM服務業指數表現 ，內需活動維持
穩健，企業投入成本壓力亦有所升溫；另一
方面，受地緣政治因素影響 ，美國柴油價格
大幅攀升 ，而柴油為卡車運輸 、農業及製造
業的重要燃料 ，若價格持續維持高檔 ，恐進
一步增添通膨壓力 。考量聯準會偏鷹立場 ，
且當前通膨降溫進程不如預期 ，年底前仍保
有升息空間。
► 美國債務總額突破40兆美元，預估2026年公
眾持有聯邦債務占 GDP的比率將達 101%，
財政可持續性風險升溫 。與此同時 ，AI基礎

建設舉債熱潮 ，疊加中東局勢動盪帶來的通
膨不確定性 ，迫使投資人重新定價長天期債
券風險 ，要求更高的風險溢酬 。隨實質殖利
率上行，美債10年期殖利率揚升並持續高懸
於5%水準之上，30年期長債殖利率亦同步站
上近20年來高點 。隨長天期債券利率續揚 ，
殖利率曲線趨陡峭 ，債券配置宜先避開長天
期部位，聚焦中短天期債券鎖利機會。

### Page 6

6
投資焦點
► 距離美國期中選舉投票僅剩不到1個月，觀察
Polymarket賭盤預估數據顯示 ，眾議院選情
較為明朗，民主黨有超過9成的機率控制眾議
院。但參議院選情則尚有變數 ，今年以來兩
黨均互有領先 ，雖然數據顯示民主黨掌控參
議院機率呈現上揚 ，然隨時序進入選前最後
衝刺期 ，參議院能否亦由民主黨掌控仍待觀
察。故就選前而言 ，由於不同政黨的政策傾
向差異 ，潛在受惠產業亦有不同 ，選舉結果
的不確定性恐令投資人不易大舉押注股市。
► 另一方面 ，油價高檔徘迴 ，導致年底前通 膨
擔憂仍存 ，加上AI企業發債需求成長 、美國
財政體質惡化等 因素共同發酵 ，推動近期 美
債殖利率逐步攀高 ，並令股市預期本益 比壓
力延續 ，進而限縮了投資人短期對美股的追
價動能，短期指數恐維持震盪輪動格局 。可
留意抗波動 、體質佳且具價值優勢之標的 ，
待期中選舉結束與3Q26財報展開後，不確定
性減退將有助資金回歸長線AI題材。
殖利率攀升令美股本益比面臨壓力參議院期中選舉選情仍具變數
期中選前擾動與殖利率走高，美股短期恐仍震盪輪動

### Page 7

7
投資焦點日股
► 9月日本銀行決議升息 1碼，明確表示潛在
CPI已接近2%，未來也將繼續調高利率 ，政
策重點開始轉向防範通膨向上偏離目標 。然
因有2位委員反對升息，加上總裁植田和男會
後談話偏向中性 ，令市場解讀此次升息未如
預期鷹派，美日利差不易快速縮窄，故9月會
議後日元反而呈現利多出盡的回貶反應。
► 觀察今年以來日股面臨短線日圓升值的狀態
並未出現顯著走弱 態勢。一方面反映企業治
理改革推動股東報酬 提升已成為 支撐股價的
重要結構性因素 。另一方面 ，升息代表日本
經濟已具備一定的健康程度 ，貨幣政策正常
化將不致對需求帶來衝擊 。因此 ，隨BoJ延
續升息方向 ，將擴大銀行淨利差 ，改善其獲
利結構，並提升金融資產收益 。儘管近期整
體日股預期獲利成長走勢略顯停滯 ，然日本
銀行股 在總 體經 濟 與貨幣 政策環 境的 支持
下，獲利成長有望逆勢攀升 ，料將成為後續
資金關注重點。
日圓升值對日股影響似乎有限，關注銀行股獲利走強BoJ升息方向延續，惟其步伐仍取決於經濟與物價

BoJ政策保持正常化路線，日本銀行股有望持續受惠

### Page 8

8
股市
 公債殖利率走高雖可能壓抑 股債市場近期 動能，然而觀察美股第三季獲利年增 率有望仍為雙位數成
長，或支撐股市表現 。市場持續關注AI資本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場
動盪，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評
價面壓力等紛擾 帶來干擾，可布局防禦 股增強抗震力 。長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且
基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融及醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨 發展。殖利率偏高環境建議以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走
升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸

引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 短期抗波動，逢回可分批布局仍具長線趨勢之AI與科技產業

### Page 15

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
•  Share markets rallied across much of the globe. In the
US, reports of an upgraded outlook for CoreWeave, an
oversubscribed IPO for Anthropic and a stabilisation in
Nvidia supported renewed optimism around AI which

buoyed tech stocks, seeing the S&P 500 (+1.4%) and
Nasdaq (+1.7%) both rally, though the smaller gain for the
Dow Jones (+0.5%) shows the rally was more narrowly
based.
•  Across the pond, the Euro Stoxx 50 continued to build on
recent momentum with another solid gain (+1.0%), while
London’s FTSE 100 bounced back from a modest decline
yesterday (+0.8%). Stocks were up across most of Asia,
including Tokyo (+1.3%), Shanghai (+0.4%) and Seoul
(+2.6%), with only the Hang Seng (–1.0%) bucking the

wider trend. The ASX 200 rose 0.3% yesterday, but weaker
trading in futures overnight points to a mixed open this
morning.
•  Cash trading in bond markets were closed in the US for
Veterans Day, but trading in futures points to a continued
rise in yields following hotter-than-expected inflation
data received earlier in the week, which all but cemented
a December FOMC rate hike. Beyond December, markets
are also pricing in another two rate hikes over the course of

2027.
• Yields were little changed elsewhere, with the 10Y Bund
and Gilt steady on the day. The 10Y JGB meanwhile
continued to sell-off, with yields jumping 10bps to 2.80%
having effectively unwound the rally over late last week.
ACGBs were also weaker during local trading, seeing yields
rise 7bps on the 3Y and 6bps on the 10Y, following the
sharp rise in global yields the day prior post US CPI. Swaps
markets point to no hikes nor cuts from the RBA through to

end-2027 at present.
•  The USD received a solid bid overnight, buoyed by sharp
inflows into AI and tech stocks that improved risk sentiment.
The DXY finished 0.7% higher at around 102.9, the
strongest since April 2025. That move has pushed the Euro
down 0.6% back to a USD1.10 handle, the weakest since
May 2025, while the Yen slipped 0.7% to JPY159.9, the
weakest since the before the early-September rally. While
moves in the AUD was comparatively small overnight,

slipping 0.2% to around the USD0.6850 mark, that move
builds on recent downward momentum and has the currency
delicately positioned near 2026’s lows.
•  Oil prices rose slightly overnight, stabilising somewhat after
recent declines amid ongoing uncertainty around the future
of the US-Iran War, as President Trump downplays the
possibility of further strikes on Iran all while negotiations
show little progress. Brent rose 1.0% to US$92.4/bbl. The
rebound in risk sentiment buoyed metals, with LME copper

up 1.0% to US$13,817/mt while LME aluminium jumped
1.9% to US$3,068/mt. Gold prices meanwhile dipped 1.2%,
falling back below the US$4,000/oz mark, building on recent
momentum post US CPI which saw real yields jump higher

Document 427: Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

Threats to European diesel imports: risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased 
risk of price rises

In Europe, the possibility of a US ban on diesel exports has heightened concerns about price trends for this widely used 
fuel1, which has already risen significantly since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran. It has become a major political issue 
on both sides of the Atlantic in the run-up to decisive elections.

A particularly tight diesel market due to a shortage of buffer stocks 
Following the outbreak of the conflict, diesel prices did indeed see some of the sharpest rises on the oil market due to particularly 
tight market conditions. Since the end of February, whilst the price of a barrel of crude has risen by 46%, diesel prices have soared 
by 100% in Europe and by 76% in the United States.

The factors alleviating pressure on the refined products market, and more specifically the diesel market, were more limited than in 
the case of crude oil. Shipments passing through the Strait of Hormuz (around 17% of maritime diesel trade prior to the conf lict) 
have fallen by around 75%. Added to this is the halt in Russian exports due to war damage, which is depriving the diesel market of 
around 12% of its maritime exports. Furthermore, unlike with crude oil, China has not acted as a stabilising  force in the diesel 
market. On the contrary, the energy crisis has led to a halt in Chinese exports of refined products (including diesel) to the  rest of 
Asia from April onwards. The resumption of Chinese exports over the last two months remains fragile, given, in particular, that 
diesel stocks are at their lowest level in 15 months.

The United States has eased these tensions in the diesel market by increasing its exports of refined products (up 25% on average 
in the first half of 2026 compared with the average over the last five years). This rise in exports has required high refinery utilisation 
rate (close to maximum capacity) and, to a lesser extent, a reduction in stocks (-11% compared with the average over the last five 
years as at mid -September for middle distillates 2). All these factors have contributed to tightening the US domestic market and 
pushed retail diesel prices to record levels. Road diesel prices (US average) have risen by 68% since the end of February and by 
74% year-on-year.

A structurally high level of vulnerability in Europe… 
Against this market backdrop, characterised by mounting tensions and very limited room for manoeuvre , Europe would be 
particularly hard hit by a reduction in – or even a complete halt to – US exports. According to Eurostat, imports accounted for 46% 
of European demand for diesel in the first half of 2026, with 40% of these imports coming from the United States. Europe, which

1 Diesel accounts for 17% of the primary energy mix in the EU and 7.3% in the United States. 
2 A category of refined petroleum products comprising mainly diesel and aviation fuel.

### Page 2

has lost a great deal of production capacity in recent years, is operating its refineries at near -maximum capacity. Its room for 
manoeuvre is therefore limited.

… But the risk of a shortage has been ruled out in the short term 
The risk of a shortage, however, appears limited in the short term. The situation regarding European stocks is not comfortable, but 
it is not alarming. Commercial diesel stocks in the ARA region 3 are currently at historically low levels (around 12 million barrels), 
23% below their level at the end of September 2025. As for emergency stocks (EU Emergency Stocks), these are, according to the 
European Commission, at a sufficient level. Eurostat estim ates this figure at 270 million barrels in June 2026 (stable from a year 
earlier), representing approximately 60 days’ worth of EU consumption. Europe imports around 0.9 mb/d of diesel from the United 
States, and emergency stocks would therefore cover around 60 days’ consumption at 4.5 mb/d.

The rise in prices could be significant 
Whatever the outcome of the conflict, diesel prices are likely to continue to rise in the short term. According to our base -case 
scenario, crude oil prices are expected to remain very high in Q4 2026 due to ongoing tensions in the Persian Gulf, whilst a return 
to normal levels of diesel exports from Russia and the Middle East appears unlikely by that time.

Among the factors likely to cause future tensions, the start of the maintenance season in Europe could affect production leve ls, 
whilst demand for diesel in the United States is typically high in the autumn (agricultural harvests and pre -winter storage). I n a 
scenario where tensions remain low, the diesel refining margin (crack spread) could rise by 5% in Q4 compared with its level at the 
end of September 2026, before gradually falling back during 2027. An intermediate scenario (a 10% rise in the crack spread in Q4 
2026) would result in moderate constraints on US diesel exports and a drawdown of European stocks. Finally, an adverse scenario 
(a 35% rise in the crack spread in Q4 2026) would imply severe restrictions on US diesel exports and a delayed European response.

What impact will this have on inflation in the euro area? 
According to our estimates, the rise in diesel prices would exacerbate the expected peak in inflation in the euro area without altering 
its timing. In our baseline scenario for September 2026 (see our recently published EcoPerspective), headline inflation would peak 
at 4.1% y/y in January 2027 before gradually falling back towards 2% by the end of the year. The two least adverse scenarios 
would add only a limited amount to the peak: +0.1 pp in the first scenario (+5% for the crack spread in Q4 compared with the end 
of Q3) and +0.2 pp in the intermediate scenario (+10%), with inflation reaching 4.2% and 4.3% respectively in January 2027. 
However, there is an upside risk in the second scenario linked to the uncertain extent of the spillov er effects. In both cases, the 
main impact would be felt through fuel prices (which quickly reflect rising market prices) and would fade over the course of 2027. 
In the case of the adverse scenario (+35%), the additional inflation would be close to 0.6 pp by early 2027, pushing inflation above 
4.6%. A shock of this magnitude would, in fact, reduce businesses’ ability to absorb rising costs, which would lead to price increases 
across a broader range of goods (food, industrial goods). Subsequently, the effect would only fade gradually.

Pascal Devaux 
Economist, Middle East, Western Balkans – Energy 
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe, Eurozone, International Trade 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

3 Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp, Europe’s main hub for the storage of raw materials.

Document 432: Top of Mind - Higher Bond Yields_ How Concerning_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind - Higher Bond Yields_ How Concerning_.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs (Top of Mind)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 31
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the r ails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, pr ovided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

We provide a brief snapshot on the most important economies for the global markets

### Page 3

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Recent acceleration in the rise in global bond yields to multi-
decade highs has begun to raise questions about the 
implications for growth and markets. Both have remained 
remarkably resilient so far, with US growth in particular 
holding relatively firm and the S&P 500 and other equity 
markets near new all-time highs. But with pressure 
beginning to emerge—most notably in European sovereign 
bond markets—and yields looking unlikely to retreat soon, 
can that resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities be revealed? 
GS Head of US Rates Strategy William Marshall first lays out 
how we arrived at higher rates, underscoring the striking 
breadth of the rise, which he attributes mainly to 
fundamentals—strong growth, inflation risks, hawkish policy 
shifts, the ongoing investment cycle, and long-run fiscal 
risks—but notes that technical factors likely amplified the 
recent move. And while he ultimately expects yields to fall 
(with YE 10-year US Treasury yields of 4.75%/4.4% in 
2026/2027, respectively) as markets become more confident 
in easing inflation, he thinks elevated long-term yields should 
have some staying power, and sees continued upside risk 
from energy price and fiscal pressures.  
So, just how much of a threat do higher rates pose to the 
macro outlook? For answers, we first turn to Tobias Adrian, 
Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former 
Financial Counsellor and Director of the Monetary and Capital 
Markets Department at the IMF, Mark Zandi, Chief 
Economist of Moody’s Analytics, and GS US economists. 
Adrian sees a global economy that has so far responded to 
the yield shock much as it has to the many other major 
shocks of the past seven years: resiliently. He notes that the 
rise in yields has not significantly tightened financial 
conditions or, more importantly, disrupted market functioning 
or forced damaging deleveraging. He believes that resilience 
could persist as long as the move in yields doesn’t accelerate 
or become more disorderly. But he is concerned about 
vulnerabilities in the AI and technology complex, sovereign 
bond markets, and EMs—not to mention cyber risks—all of 
which leaves him “vigilant but cautiously optimistic.” 
GS US economists also characterize the US economy as 
resilient. Their expectation that the Fed will deliver just one 
more rate hike this year before cutting next year—consistent 
with Marshall’s expectation of lower Treasury yields—leads 
Pierfrancesco Mei to expect only a modest 0.2pp growth 
drag in 2027 from higher rates. But he finds that the drag 
could rise to a more consequential 0.5pp if yields remain near 
current levels (see pg. 9 for GS global economists Joseph 
Briggs’ and Johan Allen’s rules of thumb for the impact of 
higher yields on rate-sensitive areas across major DMs.) 
David Mericle and Mei also worry about the implications for 
the already-concerning US fiscal trajectory, which they 
believe is unlikely to trigger a classic debt crisis but is too risky 
to ignore.     
Zandi, for his part, is generally more concerned. While his 
baseline outlook is relatively sanguine, he describes the US 
economy as vulnerable rather than resilient and views the 
“distribution of possible outcomes as very flat and heavily 
skewed toward darker tail scenarios.” He sees the clearest 
softness in the labor market, with weak hiring and falling real 
wage growth for many workers weighing on consumer 
spending. And he takes little comfort from US growth 
continuing to run around 2%—roughly its potential rate—
noting that AI accounts for roughly one-third of that growth, 
leaving the non-AI economy growing at only around 1-1.5%, 
which he calls “a pretty punk economy.” Zandi also worries 
that the US fiscal situation is “as bad as I have ever seen it” 
and that higher rates are already inflicting damage on real 
estate markets and squeezing existing debtors. As these 
stresses mount, he sees elevated risk of something breaking 
in the financial system. All told, he puts the odds of the 
current vulnerabilities pushing the economy completely off 
the rails at around one-in-three.  
With banks historically at the center of the rate shocks and 
economic crises that Zandi is worried about, we then explore 
how much risk banks pose today. Adrian and GS Head of the 
Financials Equity Group Richard Ramsden both see little 
cause for alarm, explaining that higher rates should boost 
banks’ net interest income and revenues given their asset-
sensitive balance sheets. Ramsden also doesn’t expect a 
significant deterioration in credit quality absent a broader 
economic downturn and says that most banks should have 
excess capital even after accounting for rate-driven portfolio 
losses.   
But even if banks can weather higher rates, what about 
markets more broadly? GS strategists and analysts are not 
especially concerned, with Chief US Equity Strategist Ben 
Snider arguing that current rate levels shouldn’t prevent 
continued strong US earnings growth and the S&P 500 from 
climbing higher, Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam 
viewing the headwind to corporate credit from higher rates 
as manageable amid the solid growth backdrop, and US 
Financials Analyst Alexander Blostein expecting the recent 
slowdown in private market activity to rebound once rates 
stabilize. GS multi-asset strategists Christian Mueller-
Glissmann and Andrea Ferrario add that higher yields have 
strengthened the strategic case for bonds in a multi-asset 
portfolio, though strong earnings continue to favor equities 
for now. But they all warn that if rate volatility remains 
elevated, and especially if yields continue to rise from here, 
risk assets could face mounting pressures.  
All that said, with the AI boom powering the economy and 
markets, everything may come down to whether higher rates 
will derail it. Snider views this as unlikely given the strong 
balance sheets of the firms investing in AI and the expected 
profitability of these investments, with Lynam also seeing a 
high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance. Adrian also 
maintains a positive baseline for the AI economy but is 
concerned about the circularity of investment, increased 
leverage, and the sustainability of revenue growth in the AI 
complex. And Zandi again seems the most worried, warning 
that “AI could become the part of the economy that breaks” 
if rates remain at current levels for much longer.  
Allison Nathan, Editor

Higher bond yields: how concerning?

### Page 4
